# Performance baseline run 2026-10-14

Generated by `scripts/perf/report.py` from the JSON in this directory. The analysis that
reads these numbers is `docs/perf/BASELINE-<date>.md`; this file is the evidence.

## Environment

- date: `2026-10-14`
- git_sha: `4fb59462738856b0c82570ba5f8a0058970fdd76`
- git_branch: `relay/perf-counters`
- rustc: `rustc 1.99.0 (b940084d7 2026-09-28) (Arch Linux rust 1:1.99.0-1)`
- profile: `dev (opt-level 1 workspace, 3 dependencies; the profile run.sh launches)`
- kernel: `7.2.9-2-cachyos`
- cpu: `AMD Ryzen 7 9800X3D 8-Core Processor`
- cores: `16`
- mem_gib: `30.5`
- valgrind: `valgrind-3.25.1`
- perf: `perf version 7.2.10-1`
- perf_event_paranoid: `1`
- attribution: `perf (Valgrind cannot run here)`
- strace: `strace -- version 7.2`
- load_at_start: `[2.3505859375, 3.14453125, 3.49755859375]`
- container: `False`

## How to read the tables

- **p50 / p95**: wall time of one iteration of the measured region, in process, no socket unless the name says so.
- **cpu**: process CPU time per iteration (user + system, every thread). Above wall means other threads worked; below means the iteration waited.
- **allocs / bytes**: heap allocations and bytes requested per iteration, counted by a global allocator wrapper.
- **syscalls**: from a separate strace pass with fewer iterations; per iteration, every thread, counted between marks around the measured region, so not the iteration's setup or cleanup.
- **Ir**: user-space instructions retired per iteration, every thread of the process, counted by the CPU (`perf run --counters`): the p50 over the iterations, repeatable to about 0.1 %. `+ N child` is the instructions of processes the op started, such as `git`, which callgrind never counted.
- **Where the instructions go** is sampled: `perf record` took one sample per 400 009 user-space instructions inside the measured regions. Two runs put a large bucket within 1–3 points of each other: read a difference under 5 points as noise.
- **result**: size of the JSON result the op returned, which is what crosses the socket to a client.
- A row whose outcome is an error code measured that refusal path; the note beside the group says why.

## Ten times the rows

The same list queries against a store with 400 tasks, 100 notes, 100 messages and 500 extra audit rows instead of 40, 10, 10, 50. A ratio near 10 is linear in the rows; well below is a fixed cost; above is worse than linear.

## Where the instructions go

Per scenario, sampled by `perf record` (inside the measured regions only): instructions per iteration, self cost by subsystem (every bucket at or above 0.5 %), the inclusive share of a few known entry points, and the Relay functions with the largest inclusive cost below the pipeline. Inclusive figures overlap; they answer "how much of this op is X".

`child processes` is time in a process the op started; `sampled/folded/<scenario>.folded.gz` holds every sampled stack for a flame graph (inferno, speedscope, flamegraph.pl).

### `op.bus.ping` — 14.7 k instructions/iteration, 1.0 µs native p50, 753 samples

- by subsystem: sqlite **26.0%** · allocation **21.1%** · json **14.5%** · kernel/vdso **7.7%** · memcpy/str ops **6.9%** · rusqlite **6.6%** · pipeline **4.0%** · time (jiff) **2.9%** · std/core **2.8%** · hashbrown/hash **2.0%** · ids (uuid/rand) **1.9%** · harness **1.7%** · handlers **0.9%** · bus types/registry **0.7%**
- inclusive: json to_value/to_string 25.6% · sql compile (sqlite3_prepare) 19.7% · timestamp (jiff) 7.2% · sql execute (sqlite3_step) 4.0% · sql commit + WAL write 1.7%
- Relay functions: `Engine::dispatch_with_watch_state` 95.5% · `Engine::dispatch_inner` 90.4% · `Engine::register::{closure#0}` 29.0% · `value::to_value` 25.6% · `Pong::serialize` 25.1% · `time::now` 9.2%
- hottest self: `__rustc::__rust_alloc` 18.5% · `Core::push_entry` 9.0% · `__vdso_clock_gettime [[vdso]]` 7.7% · `sqlite3RunParser` 6.9% · `__memmove_avx512_unaligned_erms` 5.4%

## Memory over 20 000 mixed operations

Ten ops per round (session.list, task.list, a keystroke, a scrollback tail, task.update, notes.list, settings.set, mailbox.list, app.status, git.status). Live heap is what the allocator wrapper has handed out and not been given back; RSS is the process; store is the SQLite file.

| ops | live heap | RSS | store | threads |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | 18.9 MiB | 31.3 MiB | 0.6 MiB | 7 |
| 2000 | 19.8 MiB | 33.5 MiB | 1.0 MiB | 8 |
| 4000 | 20.7 MiB | 35.6 MiB | 1.4 MiB | 8 |
| 6000 | 21.6 MiB | 37.4 MiB | 1.7 MiB | 8 |
| 8000 | 22.5 MiB | 39.3 MiB | 2.1 MiB | 8 |
| 10000 | 23.4 MiB | 41.0 MiB | 2.5 MiB | 8 |
| 12000 | 24.3 MiB | 42.7 MiB | 2.9 MiB | 8 |
| 14000 | 25.2 MiB | 44.6 MiB | 3.3 MiB | 8 |
| 16000 | 26.1 MiB | 46.4 MiB | 3.6 MiB | 8 |
| 18000 | 27.0 MiB | 48.1 MiB | 4.0 MiB | 8 |
| 20000 | 27.7 MiB | 49.8 MiB | 4.4 MiB | 8 |

## The engine process from the outside

A real `relay serve` (dev profile) with six fake sessions, sampled from `/proc` over 30 s windows. Wakeups are voluntary context switches summed over every thread; CPU is at kernel tick resolution.

- startup on a fresh store: **0.035 s** to the first answered ping; RSS 20.7 MiB fresh, 29.5 MiB with six sessions
- restart with six live sessions to reap: **0.005 s**; clean restarts: 0.005 s, 0.005 s

| phase | CPU % | CPU ms in window | wakeups/s | preemptions/s | RSS | threads |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| idle six quiet sessions | 0.0 | 0.0 | 3.0 | 0.0 | 29.5 MiB | 33 |
| idle resources watch on | 0.033 | 10.0 | 5.7 | 0.1 | 29.7 MiB | 34 |
| output 60 lines per s unattached | 0.0 | 0.0 | 63.2 | 2.4 | 29.8 MiB | 33 |
| output 60 lines per s attached | 0.1 | 30.0 | 216.3 | 6.3 | 31.9 MiB | 33 |

- burst of 50 000 lines (4.9 MB) to an attached client: stream quiet after **1.068 s** (includes a 1 s silence wait), 50.0 ms engine CPU, 30889 frames, 2967 dropped by the engine, 8.31 MiB on the wire carrying 4.47 MiB of the 4.67 MiB payload

| CLI call | min | p50 | max |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `relay ping` | 1.06 ms | 1.11 ms | 1.19 ms |
| `relay q session list` | 1.26 ms | 1.33 ms | 1.56 ms |
| `relay q app status` | 1.2 ms | 1.29 ms | 1.8 ms |
